+++ b/_workflows/WORKFLOW_MATCH_DAY_OPERATIONS.md
@@ -33,6 +33,13 @@ restrictive schedule foreign key cannot leave an unusable planning record.
 Match days with review or publication history, or with live operations, remain
 protected and must use schedule revision or operational correction workflows.
 
+For test cleanup or an explicitly authorized destructive correction, a closed
+match day exposes **Delete Match Day** to federation and match-day managers. The
+wizard reports the affected schedule, publication, session, deviation, and
+match counts before requiring a reason and showing a final confirmation popup.
+It removes the match-day execution and publication evidence, while retaining
+competition match records detached from the deleted publication and slots.
+
 ## Operational Control Layer
 
 The portal action-items page (`/my/action-items`) is a compact control layer

reporting).\n- **Location**: All addons live under the `odoo/` folder as individual modules prefixed with `sports_federation_`.\n-- **Primary docs**: See [odoo/CONTEXT.md](odoo/CONTEXT.md#L1), [odoo/TECHNICAL_NOTE.md](odoo/TECHNICAL_NOTE.md#L1), and the Workflows in [odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md](odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md#L1).\n\n**Architecture & Patterns**\n- **Module split**: `sports_federation_base` (master data), `sports_federation_tournament` (tournaments, matches, participants), `sports_federation_format / sports_federation_scheduling` (service/wizards), plus domain modules (`people`, `rules`, `officiating`, `rosters`, `result_control`, `public_site`, `reporting`, `finance_bridge`, `venues`, `discipline`, `standings`, ...).\n- **Model naming**: Domain models use the `federation.` prefix (e.g. `federation.player`, `federation.match`).\n- **Common patterns**: `ir.sequence` for identifiers, `state` selection fields for workflows, wizards for batch operations (under `wizards/`), `controllers/` for portal/website endpoints, and `security/` for groups/record rules/ACLs.\n\n**Key Files & Where To Look**\n- **High-level context**: [odoo/CONTEXT.md](odoo/CONTEXT.md#L1)\n- **Technical architecture**: [odoo/TECHNICAL_NOTE.md](odoo/TECHNICAL_NOTE.md#L1)\n-- **Workflows (authoritative behavioural spec)**: [odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md](odoo/_workflows/WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md#L1) and the other files inside `odoo/_workflows`.\n- **Module READMEs**: each module may have a `README.md` under `odoo/<module>/README.md` with implementation notes and expectations.\n-- **Install logs / historical notes**: `_logs/` contains installation and setup notes per module.\n\n**Development Guidelines (how to implement changes)**\n- **Add/modify a model**: create/modify `models/*.py`, export package in `models/__init__.py`, add access rules in `security/ir.model.access.csv`, add any initial `data/*.xml` and register them in `__manifest__.py` `data` or `demo` lists, add views in `views/*.xml`, update `menu_items.xml` as needed, and add tests under `tests/`.\n- **Add a wizard or service**: put transient models under `wizards/` and views under `views/` (manifest `data`), keep service logic in modules like `sports_federation_format / sports_federation_scheduling` (no persistent models if not required).\n- **Add a controller / portal flow**: use `controllers/` and add templates under `views/` or `templates/`; respect portal record rules (see `sports_federation_portal`).\n- **Security**: always add or update `security/ir.model.access.csv` and any `security/*.xml` groups/rules for new models or new endpoints.\n- **Manifest hygiene**: update `__manifest__.py` `depends`, `data`, and `version` fields when introducing DB changes or new data files.\n\n**Testing & Local Run**\n- **Where tests live**: `odoo/<module>/tests/` when present (some modules include unit/functional tests).\n- **Run module tests**: use the Odoo test runner for module-level tests (e.g. `odoo-bin -d <db> -i <module> --test-enable`) or your established local development workflow.\n- **Release sanity**: include at least one focused test for new business logic (especially schedule generation, result pipeline, and state transitions).\n\n**PR Checklist (required before merge)**\n- **Tests**: Add/adjust tests covering the new behaviour.\n- **Security**: Update `security/ir.model.access.csv` and add group/record-rule XML if necessary.\n- **Manifest**: Add new data files to `__manifest__.py` `data` list and ensure `depends` are correct.\n-- **Docs**: Update or add a short README in the module and, if behaviour changes, update the relevant `odoo/_workflows` file.\n- **Small, focused commits**: Prefer atomic commits (one logical change per commit) and descriptive commit messages.\n\n**How Copilot Should Help (recommended prompts & behaviour)**\n- **Primary rule**: Prefer conservative, minimal changes that keep module boundaries intact. Avoid touching unrelated modules unless absolutely required and explain why.\n- **When asked to implement a model change**: return a plan and a single patch that includes: the `models/<file>.py` change, `models/__init__.py` export, `security/ir.model.access.csv` entry, `views/*.xml` (if UI), `__manifest__.py` update, and a new test under `tests/` that exercises the main logic.\n- **When asked to add a wizard or generator**: create a transient model in `wizards/`, a wizard view, and a small deterministic example in tests that demonstrates expected output (e.g. round-robin schedule for 4 participants).\n- **When asked about business rules or workflows**: consult and reference the Workflow docs in `odoo/_workflows` and the `TECHNICAL_NOTE.md` to ensure behaviour matches the documented flows.\n\n**Example prompts you can give Copilot in this repo**\n- **Add a model**: \"Add a new persistent model `federation.referee` with fields `name`, `certification_level`, `active`; add ACLs, a basic tree/form view, and unit tests. Show me the patch with files changed.\"\n- **Create a wizard**: \"Create a `round_robin_wizard` that generates matches for a stage; include wizard model, view, manifest update, and a unit test verifying match count for 4 teams.\"\n- **Fix workflow behaviour**: \"Investigate why standings recompute includes contested results; propose a minimal fix and tests to prevent contested results from being counted.\"\n\n**Notes & Expectations**\n- **Authoritative sources**: the `_workflows` documents and `TECHNICAL_NOTE.md` are the source of truth for business behaviour—use them before changing workflows.\n- **Keep modules decoupled**: the architecture intentionally separates `base`, `tournament`, and the `competition_engine` service; prefer adding features in the appropriate module.\n- **Ask clarifying questions**: if a requested feature impacts tournament rules, scheduling, or publication flows, ask which workflow (`_workflows/*.md`) should govern the behaviour.\n\n**Documentation Maintenance (required)**\n\n- **Always update docs**: For any code, data model, view, workflow, or behaviour change, update the relevant documentation files in this repository as part of the same change set (or in a linked follow-up PR). At minimum, consider updating `odoo/TECHNICAL_NOTE.md`, `odoo/CONTEXT.md`, the module-level `README.md` under the affected `odoo/<module>/`, and any affected workflow files under `odoo/_workflows/`.\n- **Doc scope**: Document API/ORM changes, new models, new fields, migration notes (DB changes), public website impacts, and any user-facing behaviour changes. Include brief examples or CLI commands for running tests or sample data when useful.\n- **PR checklist enforcement**: Fail-fast — do not open a PR merging code that introduces behavioural or schema changes without documentation entries. If you cannot edit docs yourself (e.g., lacking domain clarity), leave a clear TODO in the code and notify repository maintainers.\n- **Why**: Keeping documentation current ensures maintainability, reduces onboarding friction, and prevents accidental regressions in workflows that rely on textual contracts (workflows, assumptions, and operational steps).\n\n", "content_length": 7327, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": ".github/copilot-instructions.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "7e616893749fb5ae32de33c88010309516272742120b3b28cec305c140409842", "size_bytes": 7331}

 {"content": "# Workflow: Discipline Pipeline\n\nFrom match incident through review, decision, sanctions, suspensions, and case\nclosure.\n\n## Overview\n\nWhen an incident occurs during or around a match, federation staff capture it\nas a **match incident**, group related facts into a **disciplinary case**, and\nthen record sanctions or suspensions as outcomes. The workflow is intentionally\nauditable: incidents stay linked to the case, cases carry dated decisions, and\nfinancial penalties flow into the finance bridge when enabled.\n\n## Modules Involved\n\n| Module | Role |\n|--------|------|\n| `sports_federation_discipline` | Incidents, cases, sanctions, suspensions |\n| `sports_federation_tournament` | Match context where incidents occur |\n| `sports_federation_people` | Player subject records |\n| `sports_federation_officiating` | Referee as incident reporter |\n| `sports_federation_base` | Club subject records |\n| `sports_federation_finance_bridge` | Fine amounts as finance events |\n| `sports_federation_governance` | Appeals or exceptional follow-up |\n| `mail` | Chatter on discipline records |\n\n## Step-by-Step Flow\n\n### 1. Incident Reporting\n\n**Actor**: Referee, match official, or federation staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_discipline`\n\n1. During or after a match, create a **match incident** record.\n2. Fill in the match context and at least one subject reference:\n   - match\n   - player\n   - club\n   - reporting referee\n3. Choose an incident type such as `warning`, `yellow_card`, `red_card`,\n   `misconduct`, `violence`, `admin_issue`, or `other`.\n4. Add the minute and a factual description.\n5. The incident is created in `new` status.\n6. `date_reported` and `reported_by_user_id` are recorded automatically.\n\nIncidents are visible from the match form and remain standalone until they are\nattached to a case.\n\n### 2. Case Drafting\n\n**Actor**: Disciplinary staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_discipline`\n\n1. Create a **disciplinary case** to group one or more related incidents.\n2. The case receives an automatic reference number via `ir.sequence`\n   (`FED-DISC-XXXXX`).\n3. Link the relevant incident records.\n4. Identify the subject: player, club, or referee.\n5. Assign a responsible user if the review has a named case owner.\n6. The case starts in `draft`.\n\n### 3. Review\n\n**Actor**: Case handler or disciplinary staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_discipline`\n\n1. Submit the case for review: `draft` → `under_review`.\n2. Linked incidents that were still `new` are marked `attached`.\n3. Review the evidence: incident notes, match sheet, referee report, and any\n   supporting material.\n4. If the case was submitted too early or needs corrections, use **Reopen** to\n   return it from `under_review` to `draft`.\n\nThe reopen path is only available while the case is under review. It is a\ncorrection step, not a post-decision appeal mechanism.\n\n### 4. Decision\n\n**Actor**: Disciplinary committee or case handler\n**Module**: `sports_federation_discipline`\n\n1. Once the review is complete, decide the case: `under_review` → `decided`.\n2. `decided_on` is recorded automatically.\n3. Create one or more **sanctions** and/or **suspensions** as outcomes.\n\n#### Sanctions\n\n| Type | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `fine` | Monetary penalty |\n| `warning` | Formal warning |\n| `ban` | Competition ban |\n| `point_deduction` | Standing penalty |\n| `other` | Custom disciplinary outcome |\n\nEach sanction records the target, effective date, and optional amount.\n\n#### Suspensions\n\nA suspension is a time-bound match ban for a player.\n\n- `date_start` / `date_end` define the ban window.\n- States: `draft` → `active` → `expired` / `cancelled`.\n- Active suspensions are consumed by match-sheet eligibility checks.\n\n### 5. Financial Recording\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_finance_bridge`\n\n1. When a sanction of type `fine` is created, the finance bridge can create a\n   linked **finance event**.\n2. The finance event keeps the sanction traceability through source fields.\n3. Finance events follow their own lifecycle: `draft` → `confirmed` → `settled`\n   or `cancelled`.\n\n### 6. Appeal Or Exceptional Follow-Up\n\n**Actor**: Sanctioned party, federation governance staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_governance`\n\n1. If the decision is disputed, open an **override request** through the\n   governance workflow.\n2. Mark the case as `appealed` when the disciplinary record needs to show that\n   the original decision is under formal challenge.\n3. Governance outcomes may lead to operational follow-up outside the case, but\n   the case remains the canonical history of the disciplinary review itself.\n\n### 7. Case Closure\n\n**Actor**: Disciplinary staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_discipline`\n\n1. Once the disciplinary handling is complete, close the case: `decided` or\n   `appealed` → `closed`.\n2. `closed_on` is recorded automatically.\n3. Any linked incidents that are not already closed are moved to `closed`.\n4. The case remains as the permanent disciplinary record for the subject.\n\n## State Diagram\n\n```\nIncident: new → attached → closed\n\nCase: draft → under_review → decided → appealed → closed\n              ↘ draft\n\nSuspension: draft → active → expired\n                        ↘ cancelled\n\nSanction: (no state machine — created as final)\n\nFinance Event: draft → confirmed → settled\n                                 → cancelled\n```\n\n## Integration Points\n\n| Integration | Detail |\n|-------------|--------|\n| Match form | Incidents tab is available on matches |\n| Player form | Discipline tab shows player history |\n| Match sheets | Active suspensions block player eligibility |\n| Standings | Point-deduction sanctions feed standing adjustments |\n| Finance | Fine sanctions create or update finance events |\n\n## Related Workflows\n\n- [Match Day Operations](WORKFLOW_MATCH_DAY_OPERATIONS.md) — incident capture and match-sheet eligibility effects\n- [Governance Override](WORKFLOW_GOVERNANCE_OVERRIDE.md) — appeal or exception handling\n- [Financial Tracking](WORKFLOW_FINANCIAL_TRACKING.md) — fine payment tracking\n", "content_length": 6045, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "_workflows/WORKFLOW_DISCIPLINE_PIPELINE.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "b07ab13c6f621478e86cc1b1b41fdeb9143345198324a9e7f07608ace0df4540", "size_bytes": 6093}
 {"content": "# Workflow: Financial Tracking\n\nLightweight financial event recording for registration fees, disciplinary fines,\nreferee reimbursements, and other federation-related financial activity.\n\n## Overview\n\nThe federation needs to track money flowing in and out — registration fees from\nclubs, fines from disciplinary actions, reimbursements for referees — without\nrequiring a full accounting module. The finance bridge module records these as\n**finance events** linked to their source, with states that prepare them for\nfuture accounting integration.\n\n## Modules Involved\n\n| Module | Role |\n|--------|------|\n| `sports_federation_finance_bridge` | Fee types, finance events |\n| `sports_federation_base` | Clubs (fee payers) |\n| `sports_federation_people` | Players (registration fees) |\n| `sports_federation_tournament` | Tournament context |\n| `sports_federation_officiating` | Referees (reimbursements) |\n| `sports_federation_discipline` | Sanctions (fines) |\n| `sports_federation_notifications` | Finance confirmation notifications |\n| `sports_federation_reporting` | Financial summary reports |\n\n## Step-by-Step Flow\n\n### 1. Fee Type Catalogue Setup\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_finance_bridge`\n\n1. Navigate to **Federation → Finance → Fee Types**.\n2. Create fee type entries:\n\n| Example | Category | Default Amount |\n|---------|----------|---------------|\n| Season Registration Fee | `registration` | €250.00 |\n| Player License Fee | `registration` | €25.00 |\n| Disciplinary Fine (Yellow Card) | `fine` | €50.00 |\n| Disciplinary Fine (Red Card) | `fine` | €150.00 |\n| Referee Travel Reimbursement | `reimbursement` | €75.00 |\n| Match Officials Fee | `reimbursement` | €100.00 |\n| Venue Booking (passthrough) | `other` | variable |\n\nEach fee type has a unique code, category, and default amount.\n\n### 2. Finance Event Creation\n\n**Actor**: Federation staff or automated from source modules\n**Module**: `sports_federation_finance_bridge`\n\nFinance events are created in two ways:\n\n**Manual**: Staff creates an event from the Finance Events list view.\n\n**From source modules**: Other modules create events when relevant actions occur:\n- Registration approved → registration fee event\n- Sanction with fine → fine event\n- Referee assignment completed → reimbursement event\n- Match result approved with `result_fee_type_id` → result-processing charge event\n- Match scheduled with a confirmed venue → venue booking event\n\nAll automatic hooks create source-linked events idempotently and assign a\ndeterministic `external_ref` so repeated workflow entries reuse the same draft\nrecord instead of creating duplicates.\n\nEach event records:\n- Fee type (from catalogue)\n- Amount and currency\n- Source record (via `source_model` / `source_res_id`)\n- Target entity: club, player, or referee\n- Partner (for accounting integration)\n\n### 3. Event Confirmation\n\n**Actor**: Federation finance staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_finance_bridge`\n\n1. Review draft finance events.\n2. Verify amounts, source references, and the deterministic `external_ref`.\n3. **Confirm**: state `draft` → `confirmed`.\n4. Confirmed events represent accepted financial obligations.\n5. Confirmation triggers a notification email to the resolved partner, club,\n   player, or referee contact; missing-recipient cases are logged without\n   blocking the state change.\n\n### 4. Invoicing\n\n**Actor**: Federation finance staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_finance_bridge`\n\n1. For confirmed events that need an external accounting reference:\n2. Create or look up the invoice in the accounting system (future integration point).\n3. Record the invoice reference in `invoice_ref`.\n4. Keep the bridge event in `confirmed` until it is financially settled.\n\n### 5. Payment Recording\n\n**Actor**: Federation finance staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_finance_bridge`\n\n1. When payment is received or reimbursement is completed:\n2. Set state to `settled`.\n3. Record external references if applicable (`external_ref`).\n\n### 6. Cancellation\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_finance_bridge`\n\n1. Events can be cancelled at any stage before `settled`.\n2. State → `cancelled`.\n3. Cancelled events remain in the system for audit but are excluded from totals.\n\n### 7. Reporting\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_reporting`\n\nThe **Finance Report** (SQL view-backed model) provides:\n- Summary by fee type and state\n- Total amounts per category\n- Event counts for monitoring\n- A **Finance Reconciliation** queue showing open follow-up items by event,\n    counterparty, reference coverage, and ageing\n- Report schedules that can generate weekly or monthly CSV snapshots from the\n    reporting menu without direct database access\n- CSV export via `/reporting/export/finance` for lightweight leadership dashboards\n\n## State Diagram\n\n```\nFinance Event: draft → confirmed → settled\n                     → cancelled (from any pre-settled state)\n```\n\n## Financial Event Sources\n\n| Source Action | Fee Category | Auto-Created? |\n|--------------|-------------|---------------|\n| Season registration approved | Registration | Yes |\n| Player license issued | Registration | Extendable |\n| Tournament participation | Registration | Extendable |\n| Disciplinary fine | Fine | Yes |\n| Referee assignment completed | Reimbursement | Yes |\n| Match result approved | Charge | Yes, when `result_fee_type_id` is set |\n| Match scheduled with venue | Other | Yes |\n\n> Note: The finance bridge provides the **framework** and now includes default\n> hooks for season-registration confirmation, match result approval,\n> disciplinary fines, completed referee assignments, and scheduled venue\n> bookings. Other modules can continue to create finance events programmatically via the shared\n> finance event API.\n\n## Accounting Integration Points\n\nThe module is designed to bridge to a full accounting system:\n\n| Field | Purpose |\n|-------|---------|\n| `partner_id` | Maps to `res.partner` for accounting |\n| `invoice_ref` | Links to accounting invoice |\n| `external_ref` | External system reference |\n| `currency_id` | Multi-currency support |\n| `state` (`confirmed`/`settled`) | Aligns with the current lightweight workflow |\n\n## Related Workflows\n\n- [Season Registration](WORKFLOW_SEASON_REGISTRATION.md) — registration fee generation\n- [Discipline Pipeline](WORKFLOW_DISCIPLINE_PIPELINE.md) — fine event generation\n- [Match Day Operations](WORKFLOW_MATCH_DAY_OPERATIONS.md) — referee reimbursement events\n", "content_length": 6515, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "_workflows/WORKFLOW_FINANCIAL_TRACKING.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "97921f6276831688954fd3669ecdb8c99dc6ccdad55f4de907939227a670f564", "size_bytes": 6561}
 {"content": "# Workflow: Governance Override\n\nFormal exception-request process for situations that require bending standard\nrules, with a compact decision flow and full audit traceability.\n\nCanonical scope:\n\n- This is the single exception engine for disputes, waivers, overrides, and\n   publication recovery decisions.\n- Other workflows should reference this document instead of re-describing\n   decision mechanics.\n\n## Overview\n\nStandard federation processes have rules and deadlines. When an exception is\nlegitimate, the governance module captures it as a formal **override request**\nso the justification, decision, implementation, and outcome stay visible after\nthe immediate issue is resolved.\n\n## Modules Involved\n\n| Module | Role |\n|--------|------|\n| `sports_federation_governance` | Override requests, decisions, audit notes, outcomes |\n| `sports_federation_base` | Core records referenced by requests |\n| `sports_federation_people` | Player references |\n| `sports_federation_tournament` | Tournament and match references |\n| `mail` | Chatter tracking on requests |\n\n## Common Override Scenarios\n\n| Scenario | Example |\n|----------|---------|\n| Late registration | Club missed the deadline but has a valid reason |\n| Eligibility waiver | Player needs an exception to a standard rule |\n| Result correction | Approved result requires governance-backed correction |\n| Standing adjustment | Federation needs a formal ranking correction |\n| Administrative forfeit | A match outcome must be imposed administratively |\n\n## Step-by-Step Flow\n\n### 1. Override Request Creation\n\n**Actor**: Federation official or authorised user\n**Module**: `sports_federation_governance`\n\n1. Navigate to **Federation → Governance → Override Requests**.\n2. Create a new request with:\n   - title\n   - request type\n   - target record (`target_model` / `target_res_id`)\n   - justification in `reason`\n3. The requester and request timestamp are recorded automatically.\n4. The request starts in `draft`.\n\n### 2. Submission And Withdrawal\n\n**Actor**: Requester\n**Module**: `sports_federation_governance`\n\n1. Review the draft for completeness.\n2. Submit the request: `draft` → `submitted`.\n3. If the request needs correction before a decision is made, use **Withdraw**\n   to return it from `submitted` to `draft`.\n\nThe withdraw path is only available while the request is still `submitted`.\n\n### 3. Governance Decision\n\n**Actor**: Governance officer\n**Module**: `sports_federation_governance`\n\n1. Review the submitted request, target record, and supporting context.\n2. Add audit notes if the review needs extra findings or commentary.\n3. Decide the request while it is still `submitted`:\n   - **Approve**: `submitted` → `approved`\n   - **Reject**: `submitted` → `rejected`\n4. The standard approve/reject actions create a decision record automatically.\n\nThere is no separate `under_review` state in the current model. Operationally,\nreview happens while the request remains `submitted`.\n\n### 4. Implementation\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_governance` plus the affected owning module\n\n1. If approved, implement the exception in the relevant workflow.\n2. Record the operational note in `implementation_note` when useful.\n3. Mark the request implemented: `approved` → `implemented`.\n4. Implementation writes an outcome-log row so later review can see what was\n   actually done, not just what was approved.\n\n### 5. Closure\n\n**Actor**: Governance officer or federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_governance`\n\n1. Once follow-up is complete, close the request.\n2. Only `implemented` or `rejected` requests can move to `closed`.\n\n## Audit Trail\n\nThe complete audit trail includes:\n\n- request creation with requester and timestamp\n- submission and any withdrawal back to draft\n- approval or rejection decision rows\n- audit notes with author and timestamp\n- implementation notes and outcome-log rows\n- chatter log of tracked state changes\n\n## State Diagram\n\n```\nOverride Request: draft → submitted → approved → implemented → closed\n                         ↘ draft     ↘ rejected → closed\n\nDecision: approved / rejected\n          (recorded when the request leaves submitted)\n```\n\n## Security Model\n\n| Group | Permissions |\n|-------|-------------|\n| Federation Staff | Can create override requests |\n| Governance Officer | Can review, approve, reject, and close requests |\n\n## Generic Target\n\nOverride requests use a **model/res_id pattern** and can link to many record\ntypes, including:\n\n- `federation.season.registration` — late registration exception\n- `federation.player` — eligibility waiver\n- `federation.match` — result correction or replay follow-up\n- `federation.sanction` — discipline exception handling\n- `federation.team.roster` — roster limit exception\n\n## Related Workflows\n\n- [Result Pipeline](WORKFLOW_RESULT_PIPELINE.md) — contested results may require governance action\n- [Discipline Pipeline](WORKFLOW_DISCIPLINE_PIPELINE.md) — disciplinary appeals or exception handling\n- [Season Registration](WORKFLOW_SEASON_REGISTRATION.md) — late registration exceptions\n- [Compliance Management](WORKFLOW_COMPLIANCE_MANAGEMENT.md) — compliance exceptions\n\n## Unified Recovery Model\n\nWhen operational data has already been published, handle exceptions in one\nconsistent operator model:\n\n1. Raise dispute/request exception from the owning record.\n2. Approve or reject through Governance Override.\n3. If approved, implement the owning-record correction.\n4. Re-run result and standings pipeline as needed.\n5. Recover publication state by republishing the affected standings/tournament\n   surfaces.\n", "content_length": 5608, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "_workflows/WORKFLOW_GOVERNANCE_OVERRIDE.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "fd351ce6d78617aa495dcb3799c140394c890b3a3714a1a19df7bb075fb08074", "size_bytes": 5652}
-{"content": "# Workflow: Match Day Operations\n\nEverything that happens around a single match — roster management, match-sheet\nsubmission, referee assignment, and match execution.\n\n## Overview\n\nOn match day, several parallel preparation streams converge: the team rosters\nmust be active, match sheets must be submitted with eligible players, referees\nmust be assigned and confirmed, and the venue must be set. This workflow covers\nthe operational sequence from pre-match preparation through to the final whistle.\n\nPrimary surfaces by phase:\n\n- **Preparation** — start from the portal **Club Operations Workspace** or team\n   workspace for squad sheets and roster readiness. The **Preparation Queue** is\n   a direct shortcut when the operator already knows they only need pre-kick-off\n   tasks, and the backend **Gameday Planner** is the staff-only day-level\n   surface for slot assignment and final one-day adjustments.\n- **Live operations** — use the **Live Operations Board** at\n   `/sports/competitions/<edition-slug>/operations` once matches are underway.\n- **Result follow-up** — use the portal **Result Follow-Up Queue** after full\n   time when results still need approval, contests, or standings follow-up.\n   Backend match and tournament records are specialist follow-up surfaces, not\n   the default club starting point.\n\nDirect queues and record pages exist as shortcuts, but they should not be\ntreated as equal-weight starting points for club representatives.\n\n## Operational Control Layer\n\nThe portal action-items page (`/my/action-items`) is a compact control layer\nover the preparation and result follow-up phases. It projects unresolved work\nfrom the authoritative registration, roster, club-duty, and result records\ninto `federation.operation.task` records. Re-running synchronization is\nidempotent; resolving a source closes its task, while a newly blocking source\nstate reopens the same task by its stable source key.\n\nClub representatives receive only tasks for clubs they represent. Federation\nmanagers use the backend Operational Action Queue to filter blocking work by\nclub, tournament, type, priority, deadline, and state. A task acknowledgement\ndoes not override a sporting blocker; the linked domain action remains the\nrequired resolution path.\n\n## Modules Involved\n\n| Module | Role |\n|--------|------|\n| `sports_federation_rosters` | Season rosters and match-day squad sheets |\n| `sports_federation_officiating` | Referee assignment to matches |\n| `sports_federation_venues` | Venue and playing-area assignment |\n| `sports_federation_tournament` | Match record management |\n| `sports_federation_people` | Player eligibility verification |\n| `sports_federation_rules` | Squad-size limits and eligibility rules |\n| `sports_federation_discipline` | Suspension checks |\n| `sports_federation_notifications` | Referee assignment emails and staffing alert activities |\n\n## Step-by-Step Flow\n\n### 1. Roster Preparation (Pre-Season)\n\n**Actor**: Club administrator or federation staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_rosters`\n\n1. Create a **team roster** for the season/competition.\n2. Link to the team, season, and optionally the competition and rule set.\n3. Add **roster lines** — each line links a player and can mark captain or vice-captain responsibilities.\n4. Validate squad size and player eligibility against the rule set, including\n   season registration, suspensions, and season/club-scoped license checks.\n5. Portal roster editing only offers licenses that match the selected roster,\n   season, and player, and manually posted hidden ids are rejected by the same\n   server-side scope.\n6. Review readiness feedback and set roster status: `draft` → `active`.\n7. An active roster is the pool from which match sheets draw players.\n\n### 2. Match Sheet Creation\n\n**Actor**: Club administrator or team manager\n**Module**: `sports_federation_rosters`\n\n1. For each match, create a **match sheet** for each participating team (home/away).\n2. Link the match sheet to the match, team, and source roster.\n3. Set the side (`home` or `away`).\n4. Add **match sheet lines** — typically by selecting active roster lines from\n   the team's source roster.\n5. Mark starters vs. substitutes.\n6. Assign jersey numbers and captaincy where needed.\n7. Add coach and manager names.\n8. Validate: selected players must belong to active roster lines for the same\n   team, satisfy license and registration rules for the match context, and any\n   blockers are shown as readable feedback before submission.\n9. If a submitted sheet needs correction, use **Reset to Draft**, update the\n   lineup, and resubmit. Only `submitted` sheets can take this correction path.\n10. After approval, the sheet lineup is frozen; only substitution timing fields\n    remain editable until the sheet is explicitly locked.\n\nMatch sheet states: `draft` → `submitted` → `approved` → `locked`.\n\nPortal workspace note:\n\n- Club representatives using the tournament workspace only see match-day tasks\n   for active tournaments (`open` or `in_progress`) and for teams inside their\n   current whole-club scope or explicit current team scope. Inactive or expired\n   representative rows do not keep match-day visibility alive.\n\n### 3. Referee Assignment\n\n**Actor**: Federation referee coordinator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_officiating`\n\n1. Select referees from the registry based on certification level and availability.\n2. Create **match referee** assignments with defined roles:\n   - `head` — Main referee\n   - `assistant_1` / `assistant_2` — Assistant referees\n   - `fourth` — Fourth official\n   - `table` — Table or desk official\n3. Each assignment has a state: `draft` (displayed as Assigned) → `confirmed` → `done` / `cancelled`.\n4. Assignments inherit a 48-hour confirmation deadline from the scheduled match time and stay visible as overdue until confirmed, cancelled, or the match closes.\n5. Confirmation is blocked if the official is inactive, their certification is missing / expired for the match date, or another assignment overlaps the same match window.\n6. Matches aggregate readiness from the rule set's `referee_required_count`, confirmed assignments, overdue confirmations, and assignment-level readiness issues.\n7. Scheduling and schedule approval apply these readiness rules before publication: double-booked officials block assignment moves while uncovered availability remains a warning.\n8. Creating an assignment sends an email to the assigned referee with the role and confirmation deadline.\n9. The scheduled notification scan creates federation-manager activities for overdue confirmations and staffing shortages.\n10. SQL constraint prevents duplicate (match, referee, role) combinations.\n11. The rule set's `referee_required_count` indicates how many officials are needed.\n\nFor automatic club-duty allocation, a club is considered to be playing during\nthe target match whenever one of its teams is assigned to a published match\nwhose time window overlaps the target window, even when the matches use\ndifferent courts. The allocator therefore prefers an eligible club outside\nthe whole concurrent set, not merely outside the target match's two clubs.\n\n### 4. Venue Confirmation\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_venues`\n\n1. Confirm the match venue is set (via `venue_id` on the match).\n2. Verify the playing area is available and suitable.\n   - Calendar capacity preparation excludes unavailable venue or\n      playing-area windows.\n    - Scheduling validates the selected court against the effective division\n       requirements before assignment.\n3. Contact venue via stored contact details if needed.\n4. If the venue incurs passthrough costs, scheduling the match with a venue\n   automatically creates or reuses a draft venue booking finance event. Staff can\n   still call `match.action_create_venue_finance_event()` to adjust the default\n   `venue_booking` charge when needed.\n5. Round scheduling and constraints:\n    - Matches belong to a `federation.tournament.round` (`match.round_id`). The\n       round carries the shared calendar date and venue; each match keeps the\n       exact kickoff time and optional playing area.\n    - The venues module enforces a constraint that prevents teams in the same\n       `category` from playing the same opponent more than once inside the same\n       round. This avoids repeated pairings in one shared round block.\n    - Updating a round's venue propagates that venue to its matches. Updating a\n       round's date preserves each match's time-of-day while moving the match to\n       the round's calendar date.\n\n### 5. Pre-Match Checks\n\n**Actor**: Federation staff\n**Modules**: Multiple\n\nBefore the match starts, verify:\n\n| Check | Module | Detail |\n|-------|--------|--------|\n| Match sheets submitted | `rosters` | Both teams have approved sheets |\n| Squad sizes valid | `rules` | Within min/max from rule set |\n| Player licenses active | `people` | All listed players have active season licenses |\n| No active suspensions | `discipline` | No player on the sheet is currently suspended |\n| Referees confirmed | `officiating` | Required referee roles are filled, confirmable, and not double-booked |\n| Venue set | `venues` | Match has a valid venue assignment that avoids blackout and capability issues |\n\nOperational traceability additions:\n\n- Once a roster line is used on a submitted, approved, or locked match sheet,\n   that referenced roster line can no longer be structurally changed or removed.\n- Match sheets record substitution timing (`entered_minute`, `left_minute`) on\n   the declared squad instead of adding late lineup changes.\n- Roster and match-sheet lifecycle events are written to participation audit\n   history so club reps and staff can review what changed and when.\n\n### 6. Match Execution\n\n**Actor**: Referees, federation staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_tournament`\n\n1. Set match state to `in_progress` at kick-off.\n2. During the match, record:\n   - Substitutions (update `entered_minute` / `left_minute` on approved match-sheet lines)\n   - Incidents (yellow/red cards, misconduct) → feeds into discipline workflow\n3. At full time, enter final scores (`home_score`, `away_score`).\n4. Set match state to `done`.\n\n### 7. Post-Match Actions\n\n**Actor**: Various\n**Modules**: Multiple\n\nAfter the match completes:\n\n| Action | Module | Detail |\n|--------|--------|--------|\n| Lock match sheets | `rosters` | Sheets move to `locked` state |\n| Complete referee assignments | `officiating` | Assignments marked `done` |\n| Submit result for verification | `result_control` | Feeds into result pipeline |\n| Record incidents | `discipline` | Incidents logged during match |\n| Log finance events | `finance_bridge` | Reimbursements for assignments marked `done`, venue booking charges for scheduled venue matches, and any linked fines |\n| Recompute standings | `standings` | Updated with new result |\n\nOperational readiness additions:\n\n- Match forms expose official counts, overdue confirmations, and aggregated readiness issues so staffing gaps remain visible before kick-off.\n- The notification scan creates follow-up activities for overdue confirmations and matches that still fail the officiating readiness check.\n\n## State Diagram\n\n```\nRoster: draft ↔ active → closed → active\n\nMatch Sheet: draft → submitted → approved → locked\n                          ↘ draft\n\nReferee Assignment: assigned → confirmed → done\n                                          → cancelled\n\nMatch: draft → scheduled → in_progress → done\n                                    → cancelled\n```\n\n## Typical Timeline\n\n| Timing | Action |\n|--------|--------|\n| Pre-season | Create and activate team rosters |\n| 1 week before | Assign referees to match |\n| 48 hours before | Confirm referee assignments |\n| 24 hours before | Submit match sheets |\n| Match day | Approve match sheets, verify eligibility |\n| Kick-off | Set match to in_progress |\n| During match | Record substitutions on the approved sheet |\n| Full time | Enter scores, log incidents |\n| Post-match | Lock sheets, submit result, update standings |\n\n## Related Workflows\n\n- [Result Pipeline](WORKFLOW_RESULT_PIPELINE.md) — what happens after score entry\n- [Discipline Pipeline](WORKFLOW_DISCIPLINE_PIPELINE.md) — incident follow-up\n- [Tournament Lifecycle](WORKFLOW_TOURNAMENT_LIFECYCLE.md) — tournament-level context\n\n## Tournament Operations Board\n\nA live operations board is available at `/sports/competitions/<edition-slug>/operations` for tournament day use:\n\n- **Audience**: tournament organizers, court managers, result table volunteers, federation admins.\n- **Access**: portal users with provable tournament-scoped activity (registration, participant record, or visible match in club/team scope); internal federation staff via their normal Odoo session.\n- **Entry points**: open from the backend tournament form or from the club/team\n   workspace once preparation is complete. Do not use the board as the starting\n   surface for pre-match lineup work.\n\nPhase boundary note:\n\n- The live operations board is not the primary place for lineup preparation.\n   Stay in the Club Operations Workspace, roster pages, or preparation queue until the\n   tournament day is actively underway.\n\n### Board sections\n\n| Section | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|\n| Tournament header | Name, venue, status, match counts, refresh button |\n| Action queue | Ranked “what should I do next?” list with court, timing, owner, and one-tap selection |\n| Summary cards | Clickable filters: Now playing, Next matches, Missing results, Needs validation, Court issues, Completed |\n| Court status strip | One card per court / playing area showing blocked, delayed, live, or result-follow-up status |\n| Match list by court | Cards grouped by playing area; each card shows teams, score, state, referee, and one primary action |\n| Result entry panel | Sticky side panel on desktop and bottom-sheet task panel on compact screens for fast score entry and result actions |\n| Filters | Search by team name; filter by court, match state, result state, timeline, referee |\n\nAdditional operator guidance:\n\n- each match card surfaces a server-computed **Next step** so volunteers do not need to infer the immediate action from raw state badges alone.\n- compact screens keep the board overview first; the task panel opens only after the operator selects a match.\n- keyboard shortcuts are available for rapid desk work: `Alt + ↑ / Alt + ↓` moves between ranked tasks and `Ctrl/Cmd + S` runs the selected panel's main action.\n\n### How results are saved\n\nResult saves on the operations board call the existing result-control action methods on the server:\n\n- `action_submit_result` — submits a score for verification\n- `action_verify_result` — marks result verified by a validator\n- `action_approve_result` — approves and counts in standings\n- `action_contest_result` — contests an approved result\n- `action_correct_result` — corrects a contested result\n\nAll validation and workflow state guards remain server-side. Standings recomputation is triggered by the existing hooks in `sports_federation_result_control` on approve/contest/correct.\n\n### Known limitations and follow-up ideas\n\n- Live push (Odoo bus) is not implemented; the board polls every 60 s.\n- Referee assignment workflow is not exposed on the board; use the existing officiating views.\n- Bracket visualization and drag-and-drop court scheduling are not in scope for this version.\n- QR-code per court, offline-first result capture, and pool standings preview are follow-up ideas.\n\n\n## Schedule approval handoff\n\nBefore match-day preparation can start, the planner submits the complete working\nschedule through **Submit for Review**. A different schedule approver uses the\n**Schedule Review Queue** to request changes or approve the immutable submitted\nrevision. Approved schedules move to **Publication > Approved Schedules**, where\n**Publish Schedule** creates the live immutable publication. Match-day sessions\nmust continue to resolve their matches from that exact current publication.\nThe approver needs both the Schedule Approver group and an active\nedition-specific `schedule_approver` responsibility. Approval revalidation reads\nthe submitted schedule's calendar and venue data internally; venue or calendar\nmanagement rights are not required. Publishing the approved schedule\nmaterializes any missing operational matches for its assigned fixtures before\nlinking them to the immutable publication.\n\n## Live operations and controlled deviations\n\n1. Open **Match-Day Control** and resolve all readiness blockers.\n2. Select **Open Match Day** to create an execution session pinned to the exact\n   live publication and digest.\n3. Use **Update Court** and **Report Incident** for operational conditions.\n4. Use **Operational Schedule Change** for a move, delay, postponement or\n   cancellation. A reason is mandatory. The published slot never changes;\n   `operational_slot_id` and `operational_status` represent the live reality.\n5. Resolve incidents and complete or cancel every published match.\n6. Select **Close Match Day**. Forced closure requires a reason and retains the\n   blocker IDs in the competition event.\n\nPublication replacement is impossible while a match day is open. Different\nmatch days in the same edition retain independent live publications and version\nsequences.\n", "content_length": 17311, "filesystem_mode": "0644", "git_mode": "100644", "git_state": "tracked", "path": "_workflows/WORKFLOW_MATCH_DAY_OPERATIONS.md", "record_type": "file", "sha256": "6eb29f846060e540a36d6a94de296c44d0ecbbb7a00268b63122beb8f0353686", "size_bytes": 17397}
+{"content": "# Workflow: Match Day Operations\n\nEverything that happens around a single match — roster management, match-sheet\nsubmission, referee assignment, and match execution.\n\n## Overview\n\nOn match day, several parallel preparation streams converge: the team rosters\nmust be active, match sheets must be submitted with eligible players, referees\nmust be assigned and confirmed, and the venue must be set. This workflow covers\nthe operational sequence from pre-match preparation through to the final whistle.\n\nPrimary surfaces by phase:\n\n- **Preparation** — start from the portal **Club Operations Workspace** or team\n   workspace for squad sheets and roster readiness. The **Preparation Queue** is\n   a direct shortcut when the operator already knows they only need pre-kick-off\n   tasks, and the backend **Gameday Planner** is the staff-only day-level\n   surface for slot assignment and final one-day adjustments.\n- **Live operations** — use the **Live Operations Board** at\n   `/sports/competitions/<edition-slug>/operations` once matches are underway.\n- **Result follow-up** — use the portal **Result Follow-Up Queue** after full\n   time when results still need approval, contests, or standings follow-up.\n   Backend match and tournament records are specialist follow-up surfaces, not\n   the default club starting point.\n\nDirect queues and record pages exist as shortcuts, but they should not be\ntreated as equal-weight starting points for club representatives.\n\nDraft match days may be deleted during preparation. Any draft or\nchange-requested working schedules are removed with the match day so the\nrestrictive schedule foreign key cannot leave an unusable planning record.\nMatch days with review or publication history, or with live operations, remain\nprotected and must use schedule revision or operational correction workflows.\n\nFor test cleanup or an explicitly authorized destructive correction, a closed\nmatch day exposes **Delete Match Day** to federation and match-day managers. The\nwizard reports the affected schedule, publication, session, deviation, and\nmatch counts before requiring a reason and showing a final confirmation popup.\nIt removes the match-day execution and publication evidence, while retaining\ncompetition match records detached from the deleted publication and slots.\n\n## Operational Control Layer\n\nThe portal action-items page (`/my/action-items`) is a compact control layer\nover the preparation and result follow-up phases. It projects unresolved work\nfrom the authoritative registration, roster, club-duty, and result records\ninto `federation.operation.task` records. Re-running synchronization is\nidempotent; resolving a source closes its task, while a newly blocking source\nstate reopens the same task by its stable source key.\n\nClub representatives receive only tasks for clubs they represent. Federation\nmanagers use the backend Operational Action Queue to filter blocking work by\nclub, tournament, type, priority, deadline, and state. A task acknowledgement\ndoes not override a sporting blocker; the linked domain action remains the\nrequired resolution path.\n\n## Modules Involved\n\n| Module | Role |\n|--------|------|\n| `sports_federation_rosters` | Season rosters and match-day squad sheets |\n| `sports_federation_officiating` | Referee assignment to matches |\n| `sports_federation_venues` | Venue and playing-area assignment |\n| `sports_federation_tournament` | Match record management |\n| `sports_federation_people` | Player eligibility verification |\n| `sports_federation_rules` | Squad-size limits and eligibility rules |\n| `sports_federation_discipline` | Suspension checks |\n| `sports_federation_notifications` | Referee assignment emails and staffing alert activities |\n\n## Step-by-Step Flow\n\n### 1. Roster Preparation (Pre-Season)\n\n**Actor**: Club administrator or federation staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_rosters`\n\n1. Create a **team roster** for the season/competition.\n2. Link to the team, season, and optionally the competition and rule set.\n3. Add **roster lines** — each line links a player and can mark captain or vice-captain responsibilities.\n4. Validate squad size and player eligibility against the rule set, including\n   season registration, suspensions, and season/club-scoped license checks.\n5. Portal roster editing only offers licenses that match the selected roster,\n   season, and player, and manually posted hidden ids are rejected by the same\n   server-side scope.\n6. Review readiness feedback and set roster status: `draft` → `active`.\n7. An active roster is the pool from which match sheets draw players.\n\n### 2. Match Sheet Creation\n\n**Actor**: Club administrator or team manager\n**Module**: `sports_federation_rosters`\n\n1. For each match, create a **match sheet** for each participating team (home/away).\n2. Link the match sheet to the match, team, and source roster.\n3. Set the side (`home` or `away`).\n4. Add **match sheet lines** — typically by selecting active roster lines from\n   the team's source roster.\n5. Mark starters vs. substitutes.\n6. Assign jersey numbers and captaincy where needed.\n7. Add coach and manager names.\n8. Validate: selected players must belong to active roster lines for the same\n   team, satisfy license and registration rules for the match context, and any\n   blockers are shown as readable feedback before submission.\n9. If a submitted sheet needs correction, use **Reset to Draft**, update the\n   lineup, and resubmit. Only `submitted` sheets can take this correction path.\n10. After approval, the sheet lineup is frozen; only substitution timing fields\n    remain editable until the sheet is explicitly locked.\n\nMatch sheet states: `draft` → `submitted` → `approved` → `locked`.\n\nPortal workspace note:\n\n- Club representatives using the tournament workspace only see match-day tasks\n   for active tournaments (`open` or `in_progress`) and for teams inside their\n   current whole-club scope or explicit current team scope. Inactive or expired\n   representative rows do not keep match-day visibility alive.\n\n### 3. Referee Assignment\n\n**Actor**: Federation referee coordinator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_officiating`\n\n1. Select referees from the registry based on certification level and availability.\n2. Create **match referee** assignments with defined roles:\n   - `head` — Main referee\n   - `assistant_1` / `assistant_2` — Assistant referees\n   - `fourth` — Fourth official\n   - `table` — Table or desk official\n3. Each assignment has a state: `draft` (displayed as Assigned) → `confirmed` → `done` / `cancelled`.\n4. Assignments inherit a 48-hour confirmation deadline from the scheduled match time and stay visible as overdue until confirmed, cancelled, or the match closes.\n5. Confirmation is blocked if the official is inactive, their certification is missing / expired for the match date, or another assignment overlaps the same match window.\n6. Matches aggregate readiness from the rule set's `referee_required_count`, confirmed assignments, overdue confirmations, and assignment-level readiness issues.\n7. Scheduling and schedule approval apply these readiness rules before publication: double-booked officials block assignment moves while uncovered availability remains a warning.\n8. Creating an assignment sends an email to the assigned referee with the role and confirmation deadline.\n9. The scheduled notification scan creates federation-manager activities for overdue confirmations and staffing shortages.\n10. SQL constraint prevents duplicate (match, referee, role) combinations.\n11. The rule set's `referee_required_count` indicates how many officials are needed.\n\nFor automatic club-duty allocation, a club is considered to be playing during\nthe target match whenever one of its teams is assigned to a published match\nwhose time window overlaps the target window, even when the matches use\ndifferent courts. The allocator therefore prefers an eligible club outside\nthe whole concurrent set, not merely outside the target match's two clubs.\n\n### 4. Venue Confirmation\n\n**Actor**: Federation administrator\n**Module**: `sports_federation_venues`\n\n1. Confirm the match venue is set (via `venue_id` on the match).\n2. Verify the playing area is available and suitable.\n   - Calendar capacity preparation excludes unavailable venue or\n      playing-area windows.\n    - Scheduling validates the selected court against the effective division\n       requirements before assignment.\n3. Contact venue via stored contact details if needed.\n4. If the venue incurs passthrough costs, scheduling the match with a venue\n   automatically creates or reuses a draft venue booking finance event. Staff can\n   still call `match.action_create_venue_finance_event()` to adjust the default\n   `venue_booking` charge when needed.\n5. Round scheduling and constraints:\n    - Matches belong to a `federation.tournament.round` (`match.round_id`). The\n       round carries the shared calendar date and venue; each match keeps the\n       exact kickoff time and optional playing area.\n    - The venues module enforces a constraint that prevents teams in the same\n       `category` from playing the same opponent more than once inside the same\n       round. This avoids repeated pairings in one shared round block.\n    - Updating a round's venue propagates that venue to its matches. Updating a\n       round's date preserves each match's time-of-day while moving the match to\n       the round's calendar date.\n\n### 5. Pre-Match Checks\n\n**Actor**: Federation staff\n**Modules**: Multiple\n\nBefore the match starts, verify:\n\n| Check | Module | Detail |\n|-------|--------|--------|\n| Match sheets submitted | `rosters` | Both teams have approved sheets |\n| Squad sizes valid | `rules` | Within min/max from rule set |\n| Player licenses active | `people` | All listed players have active season licenses |\n| No active suspensions | `discipline` | No player on the sheet is currently suspended |\n| Referees confirmed | `officiating` | Required referee roles are filled, confirmable, and not double-booked |\n| Venue set | `venues` | Match has a valid venue assignment that avoids blackout and capability issues |\n\nOperational traceability additions:\n\n- Once a roster line is used on a submitted, approved, or locked match sheet,\n   that referenced roster line can no longer be structurally changed or removed.\n- Match sheets record substitution timing (`entered_minute`, `left_minute`) on\n   the declared squad instead of adding late lineup changes.\n- Roster and match-sheet lifecycle events are written to participation audit\n   history so club reps and staff can review what changed and when.\n\n### 6. Match Execution\n\n**Actor**: Referees, federation staff\n**Module**: `sports_federation_tournament`\n\n1. Set match state to `in_progress` at kick-off.\n2. During the match, record:\n   - Substitutions (update `entered_minute` / `left_minute` on approved match-sheet lines)\n   - Incidents (yellow/red cards, misconduct) → feeds into discipline workflow\n3. At full time, enter final scores (`home_score`, `away_score`).\n4. Set match state to `done`.\n\n### 7. Post-Match Actions\n\n**Actor**: Various\n**Modules**: Multiple\n\nAfter the match completes:\n\n| Action | Module | Detail |\n|--------|--------|--------|\n| Lock match sheets | `rosters` | Sheets move to `locked` state |\n| Complete referee assignments | `officiating` | Assignments marked `done` |\n| Submit result for verification | `result_control` | Feeds into result pipeline |\n| Record incidents | `discipline` | Incidents logged during match |\n| Log finance events | `finance_bridge` | Reimbursements for assignments marked `done`, venue booking charges for scheduled venue matches, and any linked fines |\n| Recompute standings | `standings` | Updated with new result |\n\nOperational readiness additions:\n\n- Match forms expose official counts, overdue confirmations, and aggregated readiness issues so staffing gaps remain visible before kick-off.\n- The notification scan creates follow-up activities for overdue confirmations and matches that still fail the officiating readiness check.\n\n## State Diagram\n\n```\nRoster: draft ↔ active → closed → active\n\nMatch Sheet: draft → submitted → approved → locked\n                          ↘ draft\n\nReferee Assignment: assigned → confirmed → done\n                                          → cancelled\n\nMatch: draft → scheduled → in_progress → done\n                                    → cancelled\n```\n\n## Typical
